# General Mathematics — Questions

Answers: [answers.md](answers.md). Question numbers match answer numbers.

## VCE Units 3 & 4

### Univariate data

#### Types of data and displays

*Exam relevance: Medium (comes up regularly in exams) · Prerequisites: Reading histograms, dot plots and stem plots*

**Q1.** A survey records the postcode of each Year 12 student at a Geelong school. Postcode is best classified as:
<!-- id: general-1-1-1 -->
*Info: Application · 1 mark · about 1.5 min · Exam relevance: Medium (comes up regularly in exams); multiple choice, as in Exam 1*

- A. categorical nominal
- B. numerical discrete
- C. numerical continuous
- D. categorical ordinal

**Q2.** The time taken (in seconds) by students to run 100 m at an athletics carnival is:
<!-- id: general-1-1-2 -->
*Info: Application · 1 mark · about 1.5 min · Exam relevance: Medium (comes up regularly in exams); multiple choice, as in Exam 1*

- A. numerical discrete
- B. numerical continuous
- C. categorical ordinal
- D. categorical nominal

- A. numerical continuous
- B. categorical ordinal
- C. numerical discrete
- D. categorical nominal

### Time series

#### Time series features and plots

*Exam relevance: Medium (comes up regularly in exams) · Prerequisites: Types of data and displays*

**Q209.** A pattern in a time series that repeats at regular, fixed intervals of one year or less is called
<!-- id: general-3-1-1 -->

- A. seasonality
- B. cycles
- C. trend
- D. irregular fluctuations

<!-- id: general-3-1-2 -->

- A. a cycle
- B. seasonality

- D. an increasing trend

<!-- id: general-3-1-4 -->

- A. seasonality
- B. cycles
- C. trend
- D. irregular (random) fluctuation

<!-- id: general-3-1-5 -->

- B. cycles

- D. structural change

<!-- id: general-3-1-6 -->

- B. cycles

- D. structural change

<!-- id: general-3-1-7 -->

**Q217.** Quarterly household electricity use in Ballarat has gradually risen over ten years, with a peak every winter quarter. The best description of the time series is
<!-- id: general-3-1-9 -->

### Transition matrices

#### Transition matrices and state matrices

*Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams) · Prerequisites: Matrix arithmetic and order; Percentages as decimals*

- D. Its elements sum to the number of states

### Exam-style questions (mixed topics)

**Q843.** A café's hot drinks and a coffee machine loan
<!-- id: general-x1 -->
*Info: Calculation, Application, Recall, Conceptual · 9 marks · about 14 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2 · Topics: Scatterplots, association and correlation, Least squares regression line, Residuals and coefficient of determination, First-order linear recurrence relations, Reducible loans and amortisation*
*Multi-part question · 9 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

A café recorded the maximum temperature, \(x\) °C, and the number of hot drinks sold, \(y\), on eight winter days.

| Maximum temperature (°C) | 10 | 12 | 13 | 15 | 16 | 18 | 19 | 21 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hot drinks sold | 182 | 170 | 166 | 151 | 149 | 137 | 130 | 118 |

**a.** Name the explanatory variable. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Find the equation of the least squares line for these data. Round the intercept and slope to two decimal places. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Interpret the slope of the least squares line in this context. *(1 mark)*

**d.** The correlation coefficient is \(r = -0.9984\). Find the coefficient of determination as a percentage, correct to one decimal place, and interpret it. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Find the residual for the day with a maximum temperature of 15 °C, correct to one decimal place. *(1 mark)*

The café borrows $12 000 to buy a coffee machine. Interest is charged at 6.6% per annum, compounding monthly, and the café repays $500 each month. Let \(V_n\) be the balance of the loan, in dollars, after \(n\) monthly repayments.

**f.i.** Write a recurrence relation for \(V_n\). *(1 mark)*

**f.ii.** Find the balance of the loan after two repayments, correct to the nearest cent. *(1 mark)*

**f.iii.** How many monthly repayments are needed to repay the loan in full? *(1 mark)*

**Q844.** Planning a school fair
<!-- id: general-x2 -->
*Info: Calculation, Application, Recall · 10 marks · about 16 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2 · Topics: Critical path analysis, Matrix arithmetic and order, Transition matrices and state matrices, Steady state and long-term behaviour*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

A school is planning a fair. The activities, their durations and their immediate predecessors are shown below.

| Activity | A | B | C | D | E | F | G |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Duration (days) | 2 | 4 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 1 | 1 |
| Immediate predecessors | – | A | A | B | A | E | C, D, F |

**a.** Find the earliest start time of activity G. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Write down the critical path and the minimum time needed to complete the project. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Find the float time of activity C. *(1 mark)*

**d.** Activity B is shortened from 4 days to 3 days. Find the new minimum completion time. *(1 mark)*

Tickets cost $5 for adults, $3 for children and $2 for concessions. The matrices below show the ticket prices and the numbers of tickets sold on Saturday and Sunday. \[P = \begin{bmatrix} 5 & 3 & 2 \end{bmatrix} \qquad N = \begin{bmatrix} 120 & 150 \\ 200 & 260 \\ 40 & 35 \end{bmatrix}\] The rows of \(N\) are adults, children and concessions; the columns are Saturday and Sunday.

**e.i.** State the order of the matrix \(PN\). *(1 mark)*

**e.ii.** Find \(PN\) and explain what its elements represent. *(2 marks)*

At the fair, each hour 80% of visitors in the food area stay there and 20% move to the games area, while 30% of visitors in the games area move to the food area and 70% stay. Initially there are 100 visitors in each area. \[T = \begin{bmatrix} 0.8 & 0.3 \\ 0.2 & 0.7 \end{bmatrix} \qquad S_0 = \begin{bmatrix} 100 \\ 100 \end{bmatrix}\] where the first row and column refer to the food area, and \(S_{n+1} = TS_n\).

**f.** Find the number of visitors in each area after one hour. *(1 mark)*

**g.** In the long term, how many of the 200 visitors are in the food area? *(1 mark)*

**Q845.** A running club's times, members and savings
<!-- id: general-x3 -->
*Info: Application, Calculation, Conceptual · 10 marks · about 15 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2 · Topics: Normal distribution and z-scores, Seasonal indices and deseasonalising, Investments with regular additions*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

The 5 km times of a running club's members are approximately normally distributed with a mean of 28 minutes and a standard deviation of 3 minutes.

**a.** Find the standardised score (\(z\)-score) of a runner whose time is 22 minutes. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Using the 68–95–99.7% rule, find the percentage of runners slower than 31 minutes. *(1 mark)*

**c.** Using the 68–95–99.7% rule, find the percentage of runners with times between 22 and 31 minutes. *(2 marks)*

The club's membership varies by season. The seasonal indices are shown below.

| Season | Summer | Autumn | Winter | Spring |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Seasonal index | 1.15 | 1.05 | 0.80 | ? |

**d.** Find the seasonal index for spring. *(1 mark)*

**e.** The club had 96 members in winter. Find the deseasonalised number of members. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Interpret the summer seasonal index of 1.15. *(1 mark)*

The club invests $5000 in an account that pays interest of 4.2% per annum, compounding monthly. It adds $200 to the account at the end of each month.

**g.** Find the value of the investment after 3 years, correct to the nearest cent. *(2 marks)*

**h.** How much interest does the account earn over the 3 years? *(1 mark)*

**Q846.** A delivery business: routes, prices and customers
<!-- id: general-x4 -->
*Info: Application, Calculation, Conceptual · 10 marks · about 16 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2 · Topics: Trees, spanning trees and shortest paths, Graphs, Euler and Hamiltonian routes, Determinants and inverse matrices, Simultaneous equations using matrices, Transition matrices and state matrices, Steady state and long-term behaviour*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

A delivery company serves five towns, A, B, C, D and E. The roads between them and their lengths (in km) are: A–B 5, A–C 3, B–C 1, B–D 4, C–D 7, C–E 8, D–E 2.

**a.** Find the shortest route from A to E and its length. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Find the total length of a minimum spanning tree for this network. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain why the network has an Euler trail but not an Euler circuit. *(1 mark)*

The company charges \(s\) dollars to deliver a small parcel and \(l\) dollars to deliver a large parcel. Delivering 3 small and 2 large parcels costs $34; delivering 1 small and 4 large parcels costs $38. This can be written as \(\begin{bmatrix} 3 & 2 \\ 1 & 4 \end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix} s \\ l \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} 34 \\ 38 \end{bmatrix}\).

**d.** Find the determinant of the coefficient matrix. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Use the inverse matrix to find \(s\) and \(l\). *(2 marks)*

Customers choose between two delivery companies, Fast (F) and Swift (S). Each month, 90% of Fast's customers stay and 10% switch to Swift; 80% of Swift's customers stay and 20% switch to Fast. Initially Fast has 600 customers and Swift has 400. \[T = \begin{bmatrix} 0.9 & 0.2 \\ 0.1 & 0.8 \end{bmatrix}, \quad S_0 = \begin{bmatrix} 600 \\ 400 \end{bmatrix}\]

**f.** Find the number of customers each company has after one month. *(1 mark)*

**g.** In the long term, how many customers will Fast have, to the nearest whole number? *(1 mark)*

**Q847.** Challenge: a car loan and a falling car value
<!-- id: general-cx1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Application, Calculation, Conceptual · 9 marks · about 14 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2; harder than most exam questions · Topics: First-order linear recurrence relations, Reducible loans and amortisation, Reducing-balance depreciation*
*Multi-part question · 9 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original challenge question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

Mia borrows $25 000 to buy a car. Interest is 7.2% p.a. compounding monthly, and she repays $500 at the end of each month. Let Bₙ be the balance after n months.

**a.** Write a recurrence relation for Bₙ. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Find the balance after 2 years, correct to the nearest cent. *(2 marks)*

**c.** How many repayments are needed, and what is the final (smaller) repayment, to the nearest cent? *(2 marks)*

**d.** Find the total interest paid. *(1 mark)*

The car cost $28 000 and loses 18% of its value each year (reducing balance).

**e.** Find the value of the car after 3 years, correct to the nearest dollar. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Write a rule for the value Vₙ after n years, and explain why it never reaches zero. *(2 marks)*

**Q848.** Challenge: forecasting seasonal sales

*Challenge question*
*Info: Calculation, Conceptual · 7 marks · about 11 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); extended answer, as in Exam 2; harder than most exam questions · Topics: Seasonal indices and deseasonalising, Forecasting with trend lines, Residuals and coefficient of determination*
*Multi-part question · 7 marks · Technology-active (CAS) · Original challenge question in the style of VCAA General Mathematics Exam 2*

A shop's quarterly sales (in thousands of dollars) have seasonal indices Q1 0.85, Q2 0.90, Q3 1.15 and Q4 1.10. A least squares line fitted to the deseasonalised sales is: deseasonalised sales = 52 + 2t, where t = 1 is Q1 of Year 1. For this line, r² = 0.81.

**a.** Actual sales in a Q2 were 63 (thousand dollars). Find the deseasonalised value. *(1 mark)*

**b.** Interpret the Q3 seasonal index of 1.15. *(1 mark)*

**c.** Forecast the actual sales for Q3 of Year 3. *(2 marks)*

**d.** The 70 from part a was for t = 6. Find the residual for this point. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Find the correlation coefficient r. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Give one reason why a forecast for Q3 of Year 10 would be less reliable. *(1 mark)*
